{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DLcHsYxgkaqT2egqGBsftw","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: paper-writing-norms\n3\tdescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\n4\tallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\n5\tscope: global\n6\tversion: 2\n7\tpriority: 9\n8\ttags:\n9\t  - writing\n10\t  - paper\n11\t  - style\n12\t  - norms\n13\troles:\n14\t  - agent\n15\t  - worker\n16\t  - paper_agent\n17\t---\n18\t\n19\t# 论文写作风格规范\n20\t\n21\t> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n22\t\n23\t---\n24\t\n25\t## 一、总体写作哲学\n26\t\n27\t### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n28\t\n29\t- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n30\t- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n31\t- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n32\t- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n33\t\n34\t### 1.2 主线统一性\n35\t\n36\t- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n37\t- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n38\t- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n39\t- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n40\t\n41\t### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n42\t\n43\t- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n44\t- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n45\t- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n46\t- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n47\t\n48\t### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n49\t\n50\t- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n51\t- 重点学：\n52\t  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n53\t  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n54\t  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n55\t  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n56\t- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n57\t- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n58\t\n59\t---\n60\t\n61\t## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n62\t\n63\t### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n64\t\n65\tAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n66\t1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n67\t2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n68\t3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n69\t4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n70\t\n71\t### 2.2 禁忌\n72\t\n73\t- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n74\t- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n75\t- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n76\t- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n77\t- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n78\t\n79\t### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n80\t\n81\t写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n82\t- 问题是否重要？\n83\t- 方法是否清楚？\n84\t- 贡献是否可信？\n85\t- 结果是否有说服力？\n86\t\n87\t---\n88\t\n89\t## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n90\t\n91\t### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n92\t\n93\tIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n94\t\n95\t1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n96\t2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n97\t3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n98\t4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n99\t5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n100\t\n101\t### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n102\t\n103\t```\n104\t现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n105\t```\n106\t\n107\t### 3.3 禁忌\n108\t\n109\t- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n110\t- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n111\t- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n112\t- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n113\t- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n114\t\n115\t---\n116\t\n117\t## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n118\t\n119\t### 4.1 目标\n120\t\n121\t让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n122\t\n123\t### 4.2 结构要求\n124\t\n125\t- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n126\t- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n127\t- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n128\t\n129\t### 4.3 禁忌\n130\t\n131\t- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n132\t- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n133\t- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n134\t\n135\t### 4.4 引用充足度\n136\t\n137\t- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n138\t- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n139\t- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n140\t\n141\t---\n142\t\n143\t## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n144\t\n145\t以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n146\t\n147\t### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n148\t\n149\t**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n150\t\n151\t- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n152\t- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n153\t- 强制检查命令：\n154\t  ```bash\n155\t  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n156\t  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n157\t  ```\n158\t- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n159\t\n160\t### 5.2 段落与句式\n161\t\n162\t- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n163\t- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n164\t- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n165\t- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n166\t- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n167\t\n168\t### 5.3 用词黑名单\n169\t\n170\t- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n171\t- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n172\t- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n173\t- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n174\t\n175\t### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n176\t\n177\t1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n178\t2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n179\t3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n180\t4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n181\t\n182\t---\n183\t\n184\t## 六、图表与视觉规范\n185\t\n186\t### 6.1 知识密度原则\n187\t\n188\t- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n189\t- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n190\t- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n191\t\n192\t### 6.2 表格规范\n193\t\n194\t- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n195\t- 小数精度全表统一\n196\t- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n197\t- Caption 必须自包含\n198\t\n199\t### 6.3 图片规范\n200\t\n201\t- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n202\t- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n203\t- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n204\t- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n205\t\n206\t### 6.4 空间效率\n207\t\n208\t- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n209\t- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n210\t- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n211\t- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n212\t\n213\t### 6.5 页面节奏\n214\t\n215\t好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n216\t\n217\t- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n218\t- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n219\t- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n220\t- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n221\t- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n222\t\n223\t### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n224\t\n225\t**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n226\t- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n227\t- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n228\t- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n229\t- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n230\t- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n231\t\n232\t**宽度决策树**：\n233\t- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n234\t- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n235\t- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n236\t\n237\t---\n238\t\n239\t## 七、投稿前通用准备\n240\t\n241\t### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n242\t\n243\t- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n244\t- **不要用**：\n245\t  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n246\t  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n247\t  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n248\t- 风险：\n249\t  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n250\t  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n251\t  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n252\t- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n253\t- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n254\t\n255\t### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n256\t\n257\t- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n258\t- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n259\t- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n260\t- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n261\t- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n262\t\n263\t### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n264\t\n265\t提交前必须逐一检查：\n266\t1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n267\t2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n268\t3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n269\t4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n270\t5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n271\t6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n272\t7. 所有专有名词有定义\n273\t8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n274\t9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n275\t10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n276\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"94491e5f-9b7c-49f9-a811-3b8b821c607d","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:05:36.073Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/agent-ml-research/skills/global/paper-writing-norms.skill.md","content":"---\nname: paper-writing-norms\ndescription: 顶会级别论文写作风格规范——章节写法（Abstract/Intro/Related Work）、去 AI 风格、视觉密度、叙事哲学。Paper Agent 和 Worker 写论文章节时必须加载。\nallowed-tools: [Read, Write, Edit, Glob, Grep]\nscope: global\nversion: 2\npriority: 9\ntags:\n  - writing\n  - paper\n  - style\n  - norms\nroles:\n  - agent\n  - worker\n  - paper_agent\n---\n\n# 论文写作风格规范\n\n> 核心哲学：**论文是一个有说服力的故事，不是实验报告。** 审稿人在几十秒内判断论文价值——如果 Abstract 和 Introduction 读起来像实验日志，论文就已经失败了。\n\n---\n\n## 一、总体写作哲学\n\n### 1.1 故事驱动，不是数据堆砌\n\n- 论文必须以\"故事线\"为核心展开：问题 → 洞察 → 方法 → 验证\n- 禁止大量实验数据堆砌——每个数字出现必须服务于叙事推进\n- 附录内容必须在正文中有明确引用且融入叙事，禁止简单堆砌\n- 如果附录数据对核心叙事有重大影响，根据附录数据调整论文 framing\n\n### 1.2 主线统一性\n\n- 当 idea 在研究过程中发生变化后，**必须重审全文逻辑**\n- 常见问题：Introduction 讲的故事 A，Method 做的是故事 B，Results 又在讲故事 C\n- 修复方式：从最终实验结论反推，重写 Introduction 的问题定义和动机\n- 每次 idea 调整后检查：Abstract → Introduction → Method → Results → Discussion → Conclusion 是否形成一条连贯主线\n\n### 1.3 专有名词必须有定义\n\n- 首次出现的专有名词/缩写必须给出完整定义和解释\n- Abstract 中使用的缩写也必须在 Abstract 内定义（如 LLM = Large Language Model）\n- 不能假设读者知道你自己定义的术语——论文从来没解释过的概念不能直接使用\n- 通用规则：如果一个非标准术语第一次出现时没有括号定义，就是写作 bug\n\n### 1.4 写作前必读同类优秀论文\n\n- 每个章节动笔前，找 **2-3 篇目标会议近 1-2 年的高分论文**作为风格参考——同类问题或同类方法皆可\n- 重点学：\n  - Abstract 怎么用最少篇幅讲完故事\n  - Introduction 怎么从大问题切入到具体贡献\n  - Related Work 怎么 positioning 自己（不是 survey）\n  - Table/Figure 怎么压缩信息密度\n- **不是抄袭**，是学习同行的故事节奏和审美——不同子领域叙事节奏差异很大\n- 实操：Paper Agent 可以从 `literature_map.yaml` 中挑 `role=foundation` 或 `role=direct-competitor` 的论文，把 `{title, venue, year, abstract}` 附在 Worker 的 task_prompt 中作为概念参考；如需学习写作风格本身，让 Worker 通过 WebSearch 取得论文链接并 fetch 全文\n\n---\n\n## 二、Abstract 写作规范\n\n### 2.1 四个必答问题（150-250 words）\n\nAbstract 必须在很短的篇幅内清楚回答：\n1. **研究什么问题** — 1-2 句话交代背景和痛点\n2. **为什么重要** — 现有方法的不足（不用写太多）\n3. **本文提出什么** — 核心贡献的关键机制（简要，不堆砌细节）\n4. **结果说明什么** — 用具体实验结论支撑贡献\n\n### 2.2 禁忌\n\n- **禁止堆砌数据**：Abstract 不是 Results 的缩写。最多引用 2-3 个关键数字，要服务于 claim\n- **禁止过度宣传**：不说 \"revolutionary\"、\"groundbreaking\"\n- **禁止写成 Introduction 的缩写**：Abstract 是独立阅读单元\n- **禁止术语堆积**：如果审稿人几十秒内判断不出价值，就失败了\n- **缩写必须定义**：Abstract 中首次出现的缩写必须展开\n\n### 2.3 审稿人视角检验\n\n写完后用这个标准自检：审稿人能否在 30 秒内判断——\n- 问题是否重要？\n- 方法是否清楚？\n- 贡献是否可信？\n- 结果是否有说服力？\n\n---\n\n## 三、Introduction 写作规范\n\n### 3.1 完整叙事线（5 段结构）\n\nIntroduction 需要从一个清晰、重要的问题切入，逐步把读者带到核心贡献：\n\n1. **研究背景**（1-2 段）：大模型在某类能力/应用中已取得进展，但仍存在一个关键瓶颈\n2. **问题重要性**（1 段）：结合真实应用、已有方法局限或当前趋势，说明这不是小问题\n3. **现有方法不足**（1 段）：系统梳理为什么不够（依赖更大模型/更多数据/更复杂推理/更高成本，或只在特定 benchmark 有效）\n4. **本文核心想法**（1 段）：我们观察到什么、提出了什么方法、为什么能解决上述问题\n5. **贡献总结**（列表）：清楚列出方法贡献、实验贡献和分析贡献\n\n### 3.2 整体叙事结构\n\n```\n现有进展 → 关键缺口 → 本文洞察 → 方法概述 → 实验证据 → 贡献总结\n```\n\n### 3.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止泛泛介绍**：不要开头就写 \"LLMs have shown remarkable capabilities...\"，要从具体问题切入\n- **禁止缺少过渡**：每段之间必须有逻辑连接\n- **禁止实验报告风格**：Introduction 不是 \"我们做了 X 实验得到了 Y 结果\" 的罗列\n- 每条 Contribution 必须有对应的 section/table 支撑\n- Contribution 列表中不能出现未在前文铺垫过的概念\n\n---\n\n## 四、Related Work 写作规范\n\n### 4.1 目标\n\n让审稿人相信：作者理解这个领域，知道自己的方法站在什么位置，清楚相对已有工作的真实增量在哪里。\n\n### 4.2 结构要求\n\n- 把已有工作分成几个清晰的主题（如模型训练、推理增强、工具调用、数据构建、评测等）\n- 每类简要说明：已有方法做了什么、解决了哪些问题、和本文的关系\n- 帮助读者理解：哪些是本文直接继承的，哪些方法不同但解决相似问题，哪些留下了本文要解决的缺口\n\n### 4.3 禁忌\n\n- **禁止论文堆砌**：不要写成 \"A proposed X. B proposed Y. C proposed Z.\" 的列表\n- **目标不是展示读了多少论文**，而是做归纳和比较\n- Contribution claim（\"To our knowledge, no prior work...\"）应放在 Introduction，不在 Related Work\n\n### 4.4 引用充足度\n\n- 引用必须覆盖：**直接相关方法、baseline 论文、评测 benchmark 论文、理论基础**\n- 任何一类缺失，审稿人会立刻看出来调研不充分\n- 引用密度参照目标会议近年录用论文的量级——明显偏少意味着 Related Work 调研不足\n\n---\n\n## 五、去 AI 写作风格（硬规则）\n\n以下模式是 AI 生成文本的典型标志，审稿人一眼能看出。**必须全部消除。**\n\n### 5.1 严禁破折号（最显著的 AI 痕迹）\n\n**这是 AI 写作最容易被识别的标志，审稿人最敏感。单列为最高优先级硬规则。**\n\n- 解释性 em-dash（—，U+2014）和 en-dash（–，U+2013）必须**全部删除**\n- 替代方式：用逗号、括号或拆分为两个句子\n- 强制检查命令：\n  ```bash\n  grep -n \"—\" paper/*.tex     # em-dash，每处人工判断是否必要\n  grep -n \" – \" paper/*.tex   # 解释性 en-dash，几乎都应删除\n  ```\n- 例外（允许保留）：表格分隔符、数字范围（`2020–2024`、`pp. 12–18`）、专有名词（`Newton–Raphson`）\n\n### 5.2 段落与句式\n\n- **禁止过度分段**：不需要每 2-3 句就换段，自然段 5-8 句是正常的\n- **减少关系从句**（which, that, who）：能用简洁直接的句子就不用从句\n- **禁止 -ing 短语结尾**：如 \"..., highlighting the applicability of...\" / \"..., demonstrating the effectiveness of...\"。这是 AI 写作最典型的标志之一\n- **禁止连续使用 \"Furthermore\" / \"Moreover\" / \"Additionally\"**：一段内最多用一个过渡词\n- **避免 \"It is worth noting that...\" / \"Notably, ...\"**：直接写要说的内容\n\n### 5.3 用词黑名单\n\n- 避免 \"delve into\" / \"leverage\" / \"utilize\" / \"facilitate\" — 用 \"explore\" / \"use\" / \"enable\"\n- 避免 \"a myriad of\" / \"a plethora of\" — 用 \"many\" / \"various\"\n- 避免 \"in the realm of\" / \"in the context of\" — 直接说\n- 避免 \"paradigm shift\" / \"novel\" 过度使用\n\n### 5.4 校对清单（4 步）\n\n1. 全文 proofread 语法和拼写\n2. 检查所有代词/指示词（this, that, it）是否指代明确\n3. **`grep \"—\"` + `grep \" – \"` 全文清零**（删除所有解释性 dash，§5.1）\n4. `grep \"ing,\"` 模式——大部分 -ing 分词短语需要改写\n\n---\n\n## 六、图表与视觉规范\n\n### 6.1 知识密度原则\n\n- **图表的信息必须紧凑**——不能为了美观而留大量白空间\n- 一张图如果只传达一个简单信息，要么缩小要么合并到其他图中\n- 能用 inline text 说清的不要占一整个 Figure\n\n### 6.2 表格规范\n\n- 表格和正文文字不能视觉重叠（检查 column 宽度和 alignment）\n- 小数精度全表统一\n- 最优加粗、次优下划线\n- Caption 必须自包含\n\n### 6.3 图片规范\n\n- 数值标签不能被线、bar、点遮住——用白色背景或偏移标签\n- 图中字体 >= 7pt（打印后仍可读）\n- 栏宽约束需匹配目标 venue 模板要求\n- 概念图/流程图要有信息量，不要画一个 trivial 的 pipeline 然后占半页\n\n### 6.4 空间效率\n\n- 顶会正文页数严格受限（具体页数查目标会议官网），每 cm² 都是宝贵资源\n- 优先使用 `\\subfigure` 合并相关图\n- Table 能 inline 就不要单独浮动\n- 图表占比与其对叙事的贡献成正比\n\n### 6.5 页面节奏\n\n好论文的页面有节奏感——text、equation、figure 交替出现，读者视觉不疲劳：\n\n- **text-equation-figure 三重奏**：重要概念用\"文字解释 → 公式定义 → 图表直觉\"三步展开\n- 连续 2 页纯文本（无 figure/equation/table）→ 穿插一个 display item 打破节奏\n- 连续 2 个 figure 页（整页被 float 占满）→ 至少隔 1 段文字再放下一个 float\n- Section heading 不孤悬页底（heading 后至少跟 2 行正文，否则强制分页）\n- 最后一页内容 ≥ 2/3 页面高度（太空显得凑数，太满放不下 references）\n\n### 6.6 表格 LaTeX 规范\n\n**Booktabs 标准（硬规则）**：\n- 用 `\\toprule` / `\\midrule` / `\\bottomrule`，禁止 `\\hline`，禁止竖线 `|`\n- 用 `\\centering` 不用 `\\begin{center}`（后者多加垂直空白）\n- 最优结果加粗（`\\textbf{}`），次优下划线（`\\underline{}`）+ 方向箭头 `↑`/`↓` 标明指标方向\n- 小数精度全表统一（如全部保留 1 位或 2 位）\n- Caption 在表格上方（`\\caption` 在 `\\begin{tabular}` 之前）\n\n**宽度决策树**：\n- ≤5 列 → 单栏宽度（`\\columnwidth`）\n- 6–8 列 → 全文宽度（`\\textwidth`，双栏模板用 `table*`）\n- >8 列 → 拆成两张表或移入 appendix\n\n---\n\n## 七、投稿前通用准备\n\n### 7.1 模板必须官方（硬规则）\n\n- LaTeX 模板**必须从目标会议官网下载**（CallForPapers / Author Instructions 页面）\n- **不要用**：\n  - 网上他人转载的复制版本（可能被改过 margin / 行号设置）\n  - 上一年的模板（参数可能变：字体、margin、行号、anonymous 选项等）\n  - 组里学长留的旧版本（来源不明、无法追溯）\n- 风险：\n  - 样式不符合 → desk reject\n  - margin 不对 → 被审稿人质疑诚信\n  - reference 格式错 → 被认为不规范\n- 流程：先查会议官网 templates → 找不到再 `WebSearch \"{venue} {year} latex template official\"` → 仍找不到汇报 Director（不要用就近年版本顶替）\n- `\\documentclass[]{}` 选项必须与目标 venue + 阶段（review / camera-ready）一致\n\n### 7.2 投稿要求逐项核对\n\n- 目标会议如要求 Checklist / Ethical Statement / Broader Impact Statement / Reproducibility Statement，必须**认真填每一项**，不要全写 N/A\n- 页数限制：查目标会议官网\n- 匿名化要求：review 版 vs camera-ready 版本设置不同\n- 行号开启（review 版常需）\n- 字体大小、margin 不得修改 sty 文件\n\n### 7.3 最终修改 10 项检查流程\n\n提交前必须逐一检查：\n1. 所有最新实验结果已全部录入\n2. 根据实验结果将叙事调至最优状态\n3. 附录所有内容在正文中被引用且融入叙事\n4. 无数据堆砌（每个数字服务于叙事推进）\n5. Abstract 和 Introduction 具有吸引力（不是实验报告风格）\n6. 全文主线统一（idea 变化后各章节逻辑一致）\n7. 所有专有名词有定义\n8. 无 AI 风格标志（dash、-ing phrases、过度分段）\n9. 图表知识密度高、无视觉遮挡\n10. Reference 覆盖直接相关方法、baseline、benchmark、理论基础\n","numLines":276,"startLine":1,"totalLines":276}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01V7ZhTvsWyzfT4FXLvw58HC","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\begin{abstract}\\\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\\\\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\\\\\\\% CAR while 99.6\\\\\\\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\\\\\\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\\\\nWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\\\\\\\,mm threshold range.\\\\nWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance in our experimental setting.\\\\nIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.\\\\nIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\\\\\\\% to 54.83\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$2.29) and $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$1.61); at $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$0.35, $p{\\\\\\\\approx}0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.\\\\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with delivering collision avoidance, nor expected to do so.\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{abstract}\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"1fb07510-b50f-41e7-956c-5d60d034dfb8","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:06:16.755Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\begin{abstract}\\\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\\\\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\\\\\\\% CAR while 99.6\\\\\\\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\\\\\\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\\\\nWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\\\\\\\,mm threshold range.\\\\nWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance in our experimental setting.\\\\nIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.\\\\nIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\\\\\\\% to 54.83\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$2.29) and $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$1.61); at $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\\\\\\\%$\\\\\\\\,\\\\\\\\pm\\\\\\\\,$0.35, $p{\\\\\\\\approx}0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.\\\\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with delivering collision avoidance, nor expected to do so.\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{abstract}\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\\\% CAR while 99.6\\\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\\nWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\\\,mm threshold range.\\nWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance in our experimental setting.\\nIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.\\nIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\\\% to 54.83\\\\%$\\\\,\\\\pm\\\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\\\%$\\\\,\\\\pm\\\\,$2.29) and $\\\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\\\%$\\\\,\\\\pm\\\\,$1.61); at $\\\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\\\%$\\\\,\\\\pm\\\\,$0.35, $p{\\\\approx}0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.\\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with delivering collision avoidance, nor expected to do so.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YHSDS7rCyRAgQue1aVS4DM","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\\\\\\\\label{sec:avoidance_source}\\\\n\\\\nFine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. Does SDF geometric supervision contribute additional avoidance beyond what demonstrations provide? We compare LoRA-only, LoRA+SDF ($\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.005$), and LoRA+SDF ($\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.01$) on Pi0.5, all evaluated at the 30K-step checkpoint.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\begin{table}[t]\\\\n\\\\\\\\centering\\\\n\\\\\\\\caption{Avoidance source attribution on SafeLIBERO. SDF at $\\\\\\\\lambda \\\\\\\\leq 0.01$ adds no measurable CAR beyond LoRA-only ($p > 0.1$); $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$ shows a marginal signal ($p \\\\\\\\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n{=}2$) that requires additional seeds. All fine-tuned models exhibit 0\\\\\\\\% frozen rate and displacement $>$\\\\\\\\,0.37\\\\\\\\,m.}\\\\n\\\\\\\\label{tab:avoidance_source}\\\\n\\\\\\\\vspace{2pt}\\\\n\\\\\\\\small\\\\n\\\\\\\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\\\\n\\\\\\\\toprule\\\\nMethod & Seeds & TSR (\\\\\\\\%) & CAR (\\\\\\\\%) & EE Disp (m) \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\midrule\\\\nPi0.5 baseline & 3 & 74.7\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,1.5 & 26.8\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,1.6 & 0.367 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\midrule\\\\nLoRA-only (no SDF) & 3 & 49.7\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,3.2 & 54.83\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,2.25 & 0.391 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.005$ & 3 & 54.0\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,3.0 & 54.5\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,2.29 & 0.387 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.01$ & 3 & 53.8\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,1.5 & 56.33\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,1.61 & 0.389 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\nLoRA+SDF $\\\\\\\\lambda{=}0.1$\\\\\\\\textsuperscript{$\\\\\\\\dagger$} & 2 & 48.0\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,1.4 & 58.25\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,0.35 & 0.394 \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\bottomrule\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{tabular}\\\\n\\\\\\\\vspace{1pt}\\\\n{\\\\\\\\footnotesize \\\\\\\\textsuperscript{$\\\\\\\\dagger$}30K checkpoint, two of three seeds; third seed in progress.}\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{table}\\\\n\\\\nTable~\\\\\\\\ref{tab:avoidance_source} shows that collision-free demonstrations alone raise CAR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$28 percentage points (26.8\\\\\\\\% $\\\\\\\\to$ 54.83\\\\\\\\%) with zero frozen episodes and displacement above 0.37\\\\\\\\,m, which confirms genuine avoidance. Adding SDF supervision produces no measurable benefit: LoRA-only CAR (54.83\\\\\\\\%) is within 0.33\\\\\\\\,pp of $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.005$ and statistically indistinguishable from $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.01$ (56.33\\\\\\\\%, $p > 0.1$; Appendix~\\\\\\\\ref{sec:appendix_null}). A Phase~2 ablation ($\\\\\\\\lambda = 0$ after step 5K) yields CAR\\\\\\\\,=\\\\\\\\,57.5\\\\\\\\%\\\\\\\\footnote{Single seed (s2); computational constraints precluded additional seeds for this ablation.}; SDF gradients produce no lasting behavioral effect. The per-task CAR ordering is preserved across all conditions (chocolate\\\\\\\\_pudding highest, bbq\\\\\\\\_sauce lowest; Figure~\\\\\\\\ref{fig:pertask_fingerprint}) and invariant to $\\\\\\\\lambda$ across a 20$\\\\\\\\times$ range; avoidance patterns reflect the demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{CAR--TSR trade-off.}\\\\\\\\label{sec:tsr_collapse}\\\\nFine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$21--25\\\\\\\\,pp (74.7\\\\\\\\% $\\\\\\\\to$ 49.7--54.0\\\\\\\\%), a consequence of distributional shift from the pre-trained prior rather than learned paralysis (all models retain $>$\\\\\\\\,0.37\\\\\\\\,m displacement and 0\\\\\\\\% frozen episodes). The SSR/TSR ratio increases from 0.33 (baseline) to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\\\\\\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}): among task-completing episodes, a larger fraction avoids collisions after fine-tuning.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Dose-response at higher loss weights.}\\\\\\\\label{sec:lambda_high}\\\\nIncreasing $\\\\\\\\lambda$ by 20$\\\\\\\\times$ to 0.1 yields CAR\\\\\\\\,=\\\\\\\\,58.25\\\\\\\\%\\\\\\\\,$\\\\\\\\pm$\\\\\\\\,0.35 at the 30K checkpoint (two of three seeds), above the null distribution (mean 54.67\\\\\\\\%, $\\\\\\\\sigma = 2.04$; two-tailed permutation $p \\\\\\\\approx 0.071$)\\\\\\\\footnote{One-tailed permutation $p = 0.036$; we report the conservative two-tailed value in the main text. A directional prior is justified because the SDF loss is designed to \\\\\\\\emph{reduce} collisions.}. This departure is suggestive but inconclusive at $n = 2$. A stepwise ablation across six checkpoints (16K--28K) shows no sustained trend (mean 55.3\\\\\\\\%, within 0.3$\\\\\\\\sigma$ of the null; Appendix~\\\\\\\\ref{sec:appendix_stepwise}), and per-task CAR ordering at 30K is preserved across all other conditions (Figure~\\\\\\\\ref{fig:lambda_car}). The response is thus dose-dependent: SDF is fully redundant at $\\\\\\\\lambda \\\\\\\\leq 0.01$ ($p > 0.1$), with a marginal signal at $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$ whose modest effect size (${\\\\\\\\sim}$3.6\\\\\\\\,pp) and limited sample preclude strong conclusions. Even at elevated $\\\\\\\\lambda$, avoidance patterns remain driven by demonstration distribution rather than geometric supervision.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\begin{figure}[t]\\\\n\\\\\\\\centering\\\\n\\\\\\\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\\\\\\\linewidth]{fig5_lambda_curve}\\\\n\\\\\\\\caption{Effect of SDF loss weight $\\\\\\\\lambda$ on CAR and TSR. $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0$: LoRA-only. Low $\\\\\\\\lambda$ ($\\\\\\\\leq 0.01$) remains within the null band; $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$ (58.25\\\\\\\\%, 2 seeds) shows a marginal departure ($p \\\\\\\\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.}\\\\n\\\\\\\\label{fig:lambda_car}\\\\n\\\\\\\\end{figure}\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.49}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"93949d09-f224-41c5-bf2e-e94835b30218","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:06:18.212Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\section{Avoidance Source Diagnosis}\\\\\\\\label{sec:avoidance_source}\\\\n\\\\nFine-tuning on collision-free demonstrations produces genuine collision avoidance: LoRA-adapted Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$28 percentage points with zero frozen episodes. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Wgbivp8QCNEmZuyJsmsGRv","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01CJx2VEgCdNZQo2V2dVKndA","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_read_only","input":{"server":"westd-45947","command":"cat /root/colliforce-vla/docs/paper/discussion.tex","account":"root"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":932,"cache_read_input_tokens":32461,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":932},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"ff808343-9a2f-48a9-991f-d01a8a6a7f21"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CJx2VEgCdNZQo2V2dVKndA","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\\\\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\\\n\\\\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\\\\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\\\\\\\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\\\\\\\sim}$28\\\\\\\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\\\\\\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\\\\\\\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\\\\\\\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\\\\\\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\\\\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\\\\\\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\\\\\\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\\\\\\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\\\\\\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Limitations.}\\\\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\\\\\\\approx}$0\\\\\\\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\\\\\\\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$21--25\\\\\\\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\\\\\\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Future work.}\\\\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\\\\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"8e6de749-d778-4365-8af9-4998c8bd427a","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:06:19.098Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"\\\\\\\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\\\\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\\\n\\\\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\\\\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\\\\\\\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\\\\\\\sim}$28\\\\\\\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\\\\\\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\\\\\\\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\\\\\\\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\\\\\\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\\\\\\\S\\\\\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\\\\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\\\\\\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\\\\\\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\\\\\\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\\\\\\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Limitations.}\\\\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\\\\\\\approx}$0\\\\\\\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\\\\\\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\\\\\\\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\\\\\\\sim}$21--25\\\\\\\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\\\\\\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Future work.}\\\\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\\\\n\\\\n\\\\\\\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\\\\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.44}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n\\nAcross 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes on two VLA model families, three findings converge: current VLA safety evaluation is structurally unreliable. Action collapse renders reported CAR illusory (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:dfcar}); EE-only oracle refinement is counterproductive (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}); and collision-free demonstrations, not geometric supervision, account for all measured avoidance gains (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Why EE-only SDF fails.}\\nTwo independent causes explain the null result. \\\\textit{(i)~Demonstration data subsumes the SDF signal.} Collision-free demonstrations implicitly encode spatial avoidance; the ${\\\\sim}$28\\\\,pp CAR gain arises entirely from this distributional shift, with per-task ordering invariant across all SDF conditions (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}). At low $\\\\lambda$, SDF is redundant; at $\\\\lambda = 0.1$, the marginal signal preserves the same demonstration-driven pattern. \\\\textit{(ii)~EE monitoring cannot prevent full-body collisions.} The SDF gradient acts on a single point while collisions occur along intermediate links. Oracle experiments confirm this: ground-truth SDF refinement \\\\emph{reduces} CAR by 6--10\\\\,pp below unrefined baselines (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:ee_only}), because pushing the EE away rotates unmonitored links through obstacles. We hypothesize that collision-free demonstration trajectories implicitly encode workspace priors through their spatial distribution: the robot learns to favor regions of the workspace that are inherently collision-free, without requiring explicit geometric supervision. These two causes are independent; effective geometric safety requires both full-body supervision and training signals beyond demonstrations.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Implications for evaluation.}\\ndfCAR corrects action-collapse contamination using only EE position logging, already available in most evaluation pipelines. The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. We recommend reporting both CAR and dfCAR; the gap quantifies contamination. This contamination spans a 4000$\\\\times$ parameter range (Pi0 Full-FT ${\\\\sim}$2.5B to Pi0+A1 ${\\\\sim}$658K trainable), a structural consequence of evaluating avoidance without verifying motion. More broadly, safety may need to operate at the planning or joint-space constraint layer, since any EE-centric loss inherits the same blind spot.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Limitations.}\\nOur evaluation covers one benchmark suite (SafeLIBERO, four tasks) and two model families (Pi0, Pi0.5). The tabletop setting with fixed-base manipulator and known obstacles may not generalize to mobile platforms and cluttered scenes. Oracle experiments used a largely frozen model (Pi0+A1, TSR${\\\\approx}$0\\\\%); testing on an actively moving policy would better isolate the structural limitation. The $\\\\lambda = 0.1$ condition has $n = 2$ seeds; the marginal departure ($p \\\\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) is inconclusive. Full-body SDF remains untested. Fine-tuning degrades TSR by ${\\\\sim}$21--25\\\\,pp, yet SSR/TSR increases from 0.33 to 0.51--0.57 (Appendix~\\\\ref{sec:appendix_ssr}); reduced exposure alone does not explain the pattern.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Future work.}\\nThree directions follow: (1) full-body SDF supervision along the kinematic chain; (2) cross-architecture evaluation on OpenVLA, RT-2, and other VLA families; (3) planning-layer safety constraints in joint space rather than EE space.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Broader impact.}\\nContaminated CAR scores allow models with no genuine avoidance to pass safety benchmarks. dfCAR exposes this gap with minimal changes to existing protocols.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MaCCA9wksL7hLr1Zb2QLru","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\\begin{abstract}\n2\tVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\n3\tOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\% CAR while 99.6\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\n4\tWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\,mm threshold range.\n5\tWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance in our experimental setting.\n6\tIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.\n7\tIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\% to 54.83\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.29) and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$1.61); at $\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$0.35, $p{\\approx}0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.\n8\tDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with delivering collision avoidance, nor expected to do so.\n9\t\\end{abstract}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"c470b545-484b-435b-9681-72152fae4749","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:07:24.383Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515/projects/diff_collision_sdf/docs/paper/abstract.tex","content":"\\begin{abstract}\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models report high Collision Avoidance Rates (CAR) on safety benchmarks, yet we show these metrics are contaminated by action collapse.\nOn SafeLIBERO, an SDF-augmented Pi0 achieves 91.5\\% CAR while 99.6\\% of its safe episodes exhibit end-effector (EE) displacement below 0.1\\,mm; the robot is frozen, not safe.\nWe present displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes sub-threshold episodes, as a corrective metric; the bimodal displacement distribution makes it robust across a $[2, 200]$\\,mm threshold range.\nWe show that end-effector-only Signed Distance Field (SDF) supervision is neither necessary nor structurally sufficient for collision avoidance in our experimental setting.\nIt is counterproductive at inference time: three ground-truth oracle variants all reduce CAR below unrefined baselines, as the SDF gradient repels the end-effector but drives arm links into obstacles.\nIts contribution during training is dose-dependent: LoRA-only fine-tuning raises dfCAR from 26.8\\% to 54.83\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.25, matching SDF-augmented training at $\\lambda{=}0.005$ (54.5\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$2.29) and $\\lambda{=}0.01$ (56.33\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$1.61); at $\\lambda{=}0.1$, two of three seeds show a marginal signal (58.25\\%$\\,\\pm\\,$0.35, $p{\\approx}0.071$, two-tailed permutation test) whose per-task avoidance pattern still mirrors the demonstration distribution.\nDisplacement filtering is necessary for meaningful VLA safety evaluation; EE-only geometric supervision cannot be credited with delivering collision avoidance, nor expected to do so.\n\\end{abstract}","numLines":9,"startLine":1,"totalLines":9}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014xqpGNqKhWTjiBfCpTFgp9","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n2\t\n3\tVision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n4\t\n5\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n6\t\\centering\n7\t\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n8\t\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n9\t\\label{fig:teaser}\n10\t\\end{figure}\n11\t\n12\tIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n13\t\n14\tWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n15\t\n16\t\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n17\t\n18\t\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n19\t\n20\t\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01\\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance ranking is preserved with and without geometric supervision. The collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n21\t\n22\tOur contributions are:\n23\t\\begin{itemize}\n24\t    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n25\t    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n26\t    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n27\t    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.\n28\t\\end{itemize}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cc231b3e-29a8-4d14-ad2d-4e0c867c7c7b","uuid":"8d1978e0-80b5-4b18-bed3-004070aa4929","timestamp":"2026-05-27T22:07:25.293Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515/projects/diff_collision_sdf/docs/paper/introduction.tex","content":"\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:intro}\n\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models must avoid collisions to operate safely in physical environments~\\cite{li2026vlasafety}. The SafeLIBERO benchmark~\\cite{hu2025vlsa} provides the first systematic protocol for measuring collision avoidance in VLA manipulation, and recent methods, including SDF-augmented training~\\cite{zhang2025safevla}, constraint-based planning~\\cite{yang2025cape}, and collision-free demonstrations~\\cite{cofreeevla2026}, report high Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). Our SDF-augmented Pi0~\\cite{black2024pi0} reaches 91.5\\% CAR across 800 episodes. The problem appears close to solved.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\linewidth]{fig1_teaser}\n\\caption{The fake safety problem in VLA collision avoidance evaluation. Standard CAR classifies a frozen robot as safe because it produces zero collisions (left). dfCAR filters out action-collapsed episodes where displacement falls below $\\tau_d$; Pi0+A1's 91.5\\% safety score drops to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (center). Pi0.5 exhibits genuine avoidance with active motion above 288\\,mm in all safe episodes, and dfCAR equals raw CAR (right).}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIt is not (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Of the 732 collision-free episodes, 99.6\\% exhibit displacement below 0.1\\,mm; TSR is 0\\% across all 800 episodes. The robot is frozen, not safe. The same pattern holds across all Pi0 configurations (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}). We term this \\emph{fake safety}: \\emph{action collapse} (near-zero actions producing zero collisions) renders reported CAR illusory, and standard evaluation cannot distinguish inaction from deliberate avoidance~\\cite{karwowski2024goodhart}.\n\nWe present a systematic investigation of this problem across the Pi0 and Pi0.5~\\cite{black2024pi0} model families on SafeLIBERO, spanning 100+ experiments and 6000+ evaluation episodes. Three findings emerge.\n\n\\textbf{Fake safety is pervasive.} We introduce displacement-filtered CAR (dfCAR), which excludes episodes below a displacement threshold $\\tau_d$. dfCAR collapses Pi0+A1 from 91.5\\% to ${\\approx}$\\,0\\% (3/732 safe episodes survive). The bimodal displacement distribution makes $\\tau_d$ robust across five orders of magnitude. Pi0.5 validates the metric: TSR\\,=\\,74.7\\%, CAR\\,=\\,26.8\\%, with every safe episode above 288\\,mm displacement and dfCAR equal to raw CAR (Section~\\ref{sec:fake_safety}).\n\n\\textbf{EE-only SDF refinement is structurally counterproductive.} Three ground-truth oracle variants all achieve \\emph{lower} CAR than unrefined baselines (73.1--76.9\\% vs.\\ 82--83\\%). The SDF gradient repels the EE but rotates arm links into obstacle volumes (Section~\\ref{sec:ee_only}).\n\n\\textbf{Collision-free demonstrations are sufficient for collision avoidance.} LoRA fine-tuning on Pi0.5 raises CAR by ${\\sim}$28 points (26.8\\% $\\to$ 54.83\\%) with zero frozen episodes; adding SDF supervision at low loss weights ($\\lambda \\in \\{0.005, 0.01\\}$) produces no measurable benefit and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ yields only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($p \\approx 0.071$, two-tailed permutation test, $n = 2$); the per-task avoidance ranking is preserved with and without geometric supervision. The collision-free demonstration distribution, not EE-only SDF supervision, accounts for all observed avoidance learning on this benchmark (Section~\\ref{sec:avoidance_source}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}\n    \\item \\textbf{dfCAR metric:} displacement-filtered CAR that separates genuine avoidance from action collapse, robust across $\\tau_d \\in [2, 200]$\\,mm.\n    \\item \\textbf{Fake safety diagnosis:} 100+ experiments across Pi0/Pi0.5 showing $>$99\\% of Pi0 safe episodes are action-collapse artifacts.\n    \\item \\textbf{EE-only SDF is counterproductive:} ground-truth oracle refinement \\emph{reduces} CAR below unrefined baselines by redistributing collisions to unmonitored arm links.\n    \\item \\textbf{Demonstration sufficiency:} on SafeLIBERO, collision-free demonstrations account for all measured CAR gains (${\\sim}$28\\,pp); EE-only SDF at low $\\lambda$ ($\\leq 0.01$) adds nothing, and at high $\\lambda = 0.1$ shows only a marginal, inconclusive signal ($n = 2$) whose per-task pattern still matches the demonstration distribution.\n\\end{itemize}","numLines":28,"startLine":1,"totalLines":28}}}
